› Message from general-purpose
  ## Single-PUT limits by store

  V = I read the primary page or source code. I = inferred.

  | Store | Single-PUT max | Source | Too-large answer | Conditional PUT |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Amazon S3 | 5 GB (V) |
  docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/upload-objects.html: "With a
  single `PUT` operation, you can upload a single object up to 5 GB in size." |
  400 EntityTooLarge (V, API_Error.md) | yes (V) |
  | S3 directory buckets | no separate figure found; same as S3 (I) | – | same
  (I) | yes (I) |
  | Tigris | not documented. Only "large objects up to 5 TB" via multipart (V,
  /docs/objects/multipart-uploads.md) | – | unknown | yes (V,
  /docs/objects/conditionals.md) |
  | Cloudflare R2 | **5 GiB − 5 MiB = 4.995 GiB** (V) |
  developers.cloudflare.com/r2/platform/limits (updated 30 Oct 2026): "5 GiB
  (single-part)", footnote: "The max upload size is 5 MiB less than 5 GiB, so
  4.995 GiB." | 400 EntityTooLarge, R2 code 100100 (V, /r2/api/error-codes, 10
  Nov 2026; that page says "5 GiB") | yes (V) |
  | GCS XML API | 5 TiB (V): "Maximum object size 5 TiB… regardless of write
  method" (cloud.google.com/storage/quotas, 17 Nov 2026) | not documented |
  **no**: If-Match and If-None-Match are documented only for GET and HEAD.
  Writes use `x-goog-if-generation-match` (V). Tigris's own test matrix marks
  GCS ❌ for both |
  | MinIO / AIStor | **5 TiB** (V): AIStor thresholds page "5 TiB for
  non-multipart upload". Source has `globalMaxObjectSize = 5 * humanize.TiByte`
  | 400 EntityTooLarge (V, cmd/api-errors.go) | yes, handled in the PutObject
  handler (V, code) |
  | Backblaze B2 | "5 GB" (V, cloud-storage-large-files: "the 5 GB limit on
  standard files…"). Exact bytes not found | not documented | **not
  documented.** A Proxmox patch (Jul 2025) says B2 rejects If-None-Match on PUT
  (V, secondary source) |
  | Wasabi | 5 GB (I): "objects larger than 5 GB" need multipart or compose
  (docs.wasabi.com operations-on-objects, Feb 2026) | not documented | not
  documented |
  | DigitalOcean Spaces | 5 GB (V): "`PUT` requests can be up to 5 GB" (limits
  page, 17 Nov 2026). PUTs through the CDN with presigned URLs are capped at
  8100 KiB | not documented | **no**: If-Match/None-Match are listed for GET and
  HEAD only (V, Spaces API reference, Jun 2026) |
  | Ceph RGW | `rgw_max_put_size`, default 5_G = 5 GiB, configurable (V,
  src/common/options/rgw.yaml.in) | 400 EntityTooLarge, checked against
  Content-Length before the body (V, rgw_common.cc, rgw_rest.cc) | yes,
  PutObject reads both headers (V, code) |
  | Hetzner | 5 GB (V, docs.hetzner.com object-storage overview, 2024-09-23) |
  unknown | not documented on that page |
  | OVHcloud | 5 GiB (V, s3-limitations, Mar 2026) | unknown | yes (V,
  s3-conditional-writes, Jun 2026) |
  | Scaleway | not found (pages returned 403) | unknown | yes, per a search
  summary (unverified) |
  | Storj | "Maximum object size per PUT operation: No limit" (V, storj.dev
  s3-compatibility) | – | not documented |
  | Garage | none documented | – | no If-None-Match handling in
  src/api/s3/put.rs (I) |
  | SeaweedFS | not found | unknown | yes, in s3api_object_handlers_put.go (V,
  code) |

  **The AWS change of 2 Dec 2025** (V, "Amazon S3 increases the maximum object
  size to 50 TB") raised the total object size from 5 TB to 50 TB. It did not
  change single PutObject: the current user guide still says 5 GB. AWS's "GB"
  here is binary; the quotas pages give the part size as "5 MiB to 5 GiB" (I
  that the single-PUT cap is exactly 5 GiB).

  ## Is 5 GiB the limit everywhere?

  No.
  - **Lower:** Cloudflare R2, at 5 GiB − 5 MiB. Possibly Backblaze B2 too, if
  its "5 GB" means 10⁹ bytes (unverified). B2 likely cannot be used anyway, for
  lack of conditional writes.
  - **Configurable:** Ceph RGW, whose default is 5 GiB.
  - **Higher:** MinIO/AIStor (5 TiB), GCS (5 TiB, but no S3-style conditional
  PUT) and Storj (no limit). On these stores the s4ql client still stops at its
  own 5 GiB.
  - **Not documented:** Tigris, the store the project tests against.
  - **Unusable for s4ql anyway:** of the 5 GB stores, DigitalOcean Spaces
  documents no conditional PUT, and Wasabi and B2 document none either.

  ## What the s4ql client does for a store with a lower limit (V, code)

  - `internal/store/s3store/s3store.go:231` refuses content over `MaxSize` (5
  GiB) before sending anything.
  - Below that, `refusal()` (lines 308–310) maps only **400 + `EntityTooLarge`**
  to `store.ErrTooLarge`, and only if the request went out exactly once.
  `internal/core/tx.go:894` then fails the commit at once.
  - The SDK sends `Expect: 100-continue` (s3 v1.114.10
  `ContinueHeaderThresholdBytes`), so a store that checks the length up front
  can refuse before the body is sent. R2 and Ceph would get the correct error (I
  for R2: nobody has seen R2 send it).
  - **Any other answer gives the wrong outcome:** a 413 (from nginx
  `client_max_body_size`, or Cloudflare zone limits in front of a Worker), a 400
  with another code, or a reset connection. These become `outcomeUnknown` (line
  263). `Tx.upload` (tx.go:872–907) then sends the same bytes again with
  backoff until the caller's context ends. The user gets an error about the
  context or an unknown outcome, never "too large", and each attempt may upload
  gigabytes.

  ## Suggested spec wording

  "A transaction whose commit object is larger than the store takes in one write
  is not committed, and its commit fails at once with an error that says so.
  The limit is 5 GiB, the most that Amazon S3 takes in one write, or less on a
  store that takes less."

  It may be worth adding to `docs/todo.md` that 413 is not mapped to too-large.

  ## What I could not verify

  - The single-PUT limits of Tigris, Scaleway, Garage and SeaweedFS.
  - The exact byte limits of B2 and Wasabi.
  - That R2 really answers `EntityTooLarge` between 4.995 GiB and 5 GiB.
  - Conditional-write support on Wasabi, Hetzner and Storj.
  - That directory buckets have the same limit as general-purpose buckets.

⏺ No, 5 GiB is not the limit everywhere. It is Amazon S3's limit for a single
  write, and stores differ in both directions:

  - Amazon S3: 5 GiB. The change of December 2025 raised the total object size
    to 50 TB, but not the size of a single write.
  - Lower: Cloudflare R2 takes 5 GiB minus 5 MiB.
  - Configurable: Ceph, with a default of 5 GiB.
  - Higher: MinIO takes 5 TiB, and Storj documents no limit. Our client still
    stops at its own 5 GiB there.
  - Not documented: Tigris, the store we test against.

  Several other stores document 5 GB too, but have no conditional writes, so
  s4ql cannot use them anyway (DigitalOcean Spaces, and probably Backblaze B2
  and Wasabi).

  So "On S3 the limit is 5 GiB" is not true for every store. I propose a wording
  that stays true:

  ▎ A transaction that is too large fails at its commit. A transaction whose 
  ▎ commit object is larger than the store takes in one write is not committed, 
  ▎ and its commit fails at once with an error that says so. The limit is 5 GiB,
  ▎ or less on a store that takes less.

  The research also found a gap in our client, which I am recording in
  docs/todo.md to fix. It only recognizes a store's refusal when it comes as 400
  EntityTooLarge. If a store or a proxy in front of it answers a too-large
  write differently, for example with HTTP 413, the client treats the outcome as
  unknown and uploads the same bytes again until the caller gives up. The
  commit never succeeds, so nothing is wrong in the data, but the user gets the
  wrong error and it wastes large uploads.

  Agree with the wording?
